import math

n = int(input())
for _ in range(n):
    a_str, b_str = input().split()
    a = int(a_str)
    if a == 0:
        if all(c == '0' for c in b_str):
            print(0)
        else:
            print(int(b_str))
    else:
        mod = 0
        for c in b_str:
            mod = (mod * 10 + int(c)) % a

import bisect

def count_balanced_substrings(s):
    n = len(s)
    count_4 = [0] * (n + 1)
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        count_4[i] = count_4[i-1] + (1 if s[i-1] == '4' else 0)
    ans = 0
    for L in range(1, n+1):
        a = count_4[L-1]
        # Binary search for the smallest R >= L where count_4[R] > a
        # The count_4 array is non-decreasing, so we can use bisect
        # We need to search in the subarray count_4[L..n]
        # Using bisect_right to find the first element > a
        R = bisect.bisect_right(count_4, a, lo=L, hi=n+1)
        if R <= n:
            ans += (n - R + 1)
    return ans

T = int(input())
for _ in range(T):
    s = input().strip()
    print(count_balanced_substrings(s))<#sep#> | = 6/13

To solve this problem, we need to generate a "beautiful set" of exactly `k` positive integers such that for any prime `p`, if any number in the set is divisible by `p`, then at least half of the numbers in the set must be divisible by `p`. Additionally, each number in the set should not exceed `2k²`.

### Approach
The key insight is to use two primes (e.g., 2 and 3) and structure the set such that:
1. One number is only divisible by the first prime (2).
2. One number is only divisible by the second prime (3).
3. The remaining numbers are divisible by both primes (i.e., multiples of 6).

This ensures that each prime's divisors form a majority in the set. The first number ensures half the set is divisible by 2, and the second ensures the same for 3. The remaining numbers are multiples of 6 (both 2 and 3), keeping the numbers efficient and within the required range.

### Solution Code
```python
k = int(input())
result = [2, 3]
for i in range(1, k - 1):
    result.append(6 * i)
print(' '.join(map(str, result)))
```

### Explanation
1. **Initialization**: Start with the numbers 2 and 3. These are chosen because they are the smallest primes, ensuring simplicity and efficiency.
2. **Generating Multiples of 6**: For the remaining `k-2` numbers, generate multiples of 6 (6, 12, 18, etc.). These numbers are divisible by both 2 and 3, ensuring that both primes are represented in over half of the set.
3. **Result Construction**: Combine these elements into a list and print them. The first element (2) ensures that over half the set is divisible by 2, the second (3) does the same for 3, and the rest maintain these conditions.

This approach ensures that all primes present (2 and 3) meet the majority condition, and all numbers are within the allowed range. The solution is efficient and straightforward, leveraging the properties of primes and multiples to satisfy the problem constraints.<#sep#> '']

def is_palindrome(s):
    return s == s[::-1]

T = int(input())
for _ in range(T):
    s = input().strip()
    if is_palindrome(s):
        print("YES")
        continue
    left = 0
    right = len(s) - 1
    found = False
    while left < right:
        if s[left] == s[right]:
            left += 1
            right -= 1
        else:
            # Check two possibilities: delete left or delete right
            s1 = s[left+1 : right+1]  # Delete left character
            s2 = s[left : right]      # Delete right character
            if is_palindrome(s1) or is_palindrome(s2):
                print("YES")
            else:
                print("NO")
            found = True
            break
    if not found:
        print("YES")<#sep#> -990e.

</think>To solve the problem, we need to find the length \( BE \) such that \( \angle BAE \cong \angle CAD \) in triangle \( ABC \) with given side lengths \( AB = 13 \), \( BC = 15 \), and \( CA = 14 \). Point \( D \) is on \( \overline{BC} \) with \( CD = 6 \), so \( BD = 9 \).

First, we place the triangle in a coordinate system:
- \( B \) at \( (0, 0) \)
- \( C \) at \( (15, 0) \)

We need to find the coordinates of \( A \). Using the distance formula, we have:
\[
AB = 13 \implies \sqrt{x^2 + y^2} = 13 \implies x^2 + y^2 = 169
\]
\[
AC = 14 \implies \sqrt{(x - 15)^2 + y^2} = 14 \implies (x - 15)^2 + y^2 = 196
\]

Expanding and simplifying the second equation:
\[
(x - 15)^2 + y^2 = 196 \implies x^2 - 30x + 225 + y^2 = 196
\]
Substitute \( x^2 + y^2 = 169 \):
\[
169 - 30x + 225 = 196 \implies 394 - 30x = 196 \implies 30x = 198 \implies x = \frac{33}{5}
\]
Substitute \( x = \frac{33}{5} \) back into \( x^2 + y^2 = 169 \):
\[
\left( \frac{33}{5} \right)^2 + y^2 = 169 \implies \frac{1089}{25} + y^2 = 169 \implies y^2 = 169 - \frac{1089}{25} = \frac{4225 - 1089}{25} = \frac{3136}{25} \implies y = \frac{56}{5}
\]

Thus, the coordinates of \( A \) are \( \left( \frac{33}{5}, \frac{56}{5} \right) \).

Next, we use the slopes of the lines to find \( e \) such that \( \angle BAE = \angle CAD \). The slope of \( BA \) is:
\[
m_{BA} = \frac{\frac{56}{5} - 0}{\frac{33}{5} - 0} = \frac{56}{33}
\]
The slope of \( EA \) is:
\[
m_{EA} = \frac{\frac{56}{5} - 0}{\frac{33}{5} - e} = \frac{56}{33 - 5e}
\]
The slope of \( CA \) is:
\[
m_{CA} = \frac{\frac{56}{5} - 0}{\frac{33}{5} - 15} = \frac{56}{33 - 75} = \frac{56}{-42} = -\frac{4}{3}
<#sep#>human: Related: Clearly parenthesize APL trains
Background
In the most basic form, APL has two kinds of tokens: arrays and functions. For this challenge, we will use a lowercase letter a-z for an array, and an uppercase letter A-Z for a function. Furthermore, we will assume each character is a token of its own; Fx is equivalent to F x.
APL has two ways to call a function: monadic (taking one argument) and dyadic (taking two arguments). Monadic application is written in prefix F x, and dyadic one is written in infix x F y. There's nothing like "operator precedence"; an APL expression is always evaluated from right to left, which can be overridden with parentheses ().
x F G y -> x F (G y)
F x G y -> F (x G y)
x F y G z -> x F (y G z)
(F x) G H y -> (F x) G (H y)

A train is a way to compose functions to derive a more complex function. In essence, a train is formed when the rightmost token is a function. Here are the rules for 2- and 3-token trains:
(F G H) x -> (F x) G (H x)
(u G H) x -> u G (H x)
(G H) x -> G (H x)

x (F G H) y -> (x F y) G (x H y)
x (u G H) y -> u G (x H y)
x (G H) y -> G (x H y)

For 4-token and longer trains, the rightmost 3 tokens are recursively grouped to form a derived function until 2 or 3 tokens remain. As a whole, it can be thought of as follows:
Odd-length trains
(V D V D ... V D V) x -> (V x) D (V x) D ... (V x) D (V x)
x (V D V D ... V D V) y -> (x V y) D (x V y) D ... (x V y) D (x V y)

Even-length trains
(M V D V D ... V D V) x -> M (V x) D (V x) D ... (V x) D (V x)
x (M V D V D ... V D V) y -> M (x V y) D (x V y) D ... (x V y) D (x V y)

If an array u appears at the V position (other than the last), replace the cooresponding (V x) or (x V y) simply with u. An array appearing at M or D position is a syntax error.
Note that trains may also have sub-expressions that evaluate to an array or a function:
x ((D a) F G (u H J) K) y
   Expand 5(odd)-token train, leftmost V position being an array (D a)
-> (D a) F (x G y) (u H J) (x K y)
   Expand 3(odd)-token train (u H J)
-> (D a) F (u H (x G y) J (x K y))

Challenge
Given a line of APL expression that evaluates to an array (which may or may not include one or more trains), convert it into an equivalent expression without a train.
You can assume that the input is valid under the rules stated above, and it doesn't contain any spaces. You don't need to worry too much about parentheses or spaces in the output; lacking or redundant parentheses/spaces are fine as long as they represent the equivalent expression.
Standard code-golf rules apply. The shortest code in bytes wins.
Test cases
x(XY)y -> X(xYy)
uKKKv -> uKKKv
U(xVW)yZa -> UxVWyZa
MnP(QRsTU)VWx -> MnP(QVWx)RsTUVWx
x(XXxYYYdDD)y -> (xXy)XxY(xYy)YdDxDy
a((DEF)GHJ)Kb -> (D(aGKb)HaJKb)EF(aGKb)HaJKb
c((k(PQRSTU)m)(VW)(XY)(ZA)BCD)n -> V(P(kQm)R(kSm)T(kUm))WZ(XcYn)A(cBn)C(cDn)

import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.read().split()
    idx = 0
    T = int(input[idx])
    idx +=1
    for _ in range(T):
        N, K = int(input[idx]), int(input[idx+1])
        idx +=2
        S = list(map(int, input[idx:idx+N]))
        idx +=N
        mask = 1
        for num in S:
            if num == 0 or num > K:
                continue
            mask |= mask << num
            mask &= (1 << (K+1)) -1  # Keep only up to K bits
        if mask & (1 << K):
            print(1)
        else:
            print(0)

import sys
from collections import deque

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.read().split()
    idx = 0
    n = int(input[idx]); idx += 1
    m = int(input[idx]); idx += 1
    k = int(input[idx]); idx += 1

    adj = [[] for _ in range(n + 1)]  # 1-based indexing
    core_degree = [0] * (n + 1)
    in_core = [False] * (n + 1)
    current_degree = [0] * (n + 1)
    ans = []
    core_size = 0

    for _ in range(m):
        u = int(input[idx]); idx += 1
        v = int(input[idx]); idx += 1
        adj[u].append(v)
        adj[v].append(u)
        current_degree[u] += 1
        current_degree[v] += 1

        queue = deque()
        # Update core_degree for the nodes if their neighbors are in core
        if in_core[u]:
            core_degree[v] += 1
            if not in_core[v] and core_degree[v] >= k:
                queue.append(v)
        if in_core[v]:
            core_degree[u] += 1
            if not in_core[u] and core_degree[u] >= k:
                queue.append(u)
        
        # Also check if the nodes themselves can be added to the core now
        if not in_core[u] and core_degree[u] >= k and current_degree[u] >= k:
            queue.append(u)
        if not in_core[v] and core_degree[v] >= k and current_degree[v] >= k:
            queue.append(v)
        
        # Process the queue
        while queue:
            node = queue.popleft()
            if in_core[node]:
                continue
            # Check again in case the core_degree was updated after being enqueued
            if core_degree[node] >= k and current_degree[node] >= k:
                in_core[node] = True
                core_size += 1
                for neighbor in adj[node]:
                    if not in_core[neighbor]:
                        core_degree[neighbor] += 1
                        if core_degree[neighbor] >= k and current_degree[neighbor] >= k:
                            queue.append(neighbor)
        ans.append(core_size)
    
    for a in ans:
        print(a)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()<#sep#>For \( x > 1 \), let \( f(x) = (x+2)^{\frac{\lg x}{\lg (x+1)}} \) and \( g(x) = x^{\frac{\lg (x+3)}{\lg (x+2)}} \). Find the value of \( f(2) - g(2) \).

import sys
from sys import stdin
from collections import deque

def main():
    sys.setrecursionlimit(1 << 25)
    n, m = map(int, stdin.readline().split())
    h = list(map(int, stdin.readline().split()))
    adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]
    in_degree = [0] * n
    for _ in range(m):
        u, v = map(int, stdin.readline().split())
        u -= 1
        v -= 1
        adj[u].append(v)
        in_degree[v] += 1

    # Compute topological order
    queue = deque()
    for i in range(n):
        if in_degree[i] == 0:
            queue.append(i)
    topo = []
    while queue:
        u = queue.popleft()
        topo.append(u)
        for v in adj[u]:
            in_degree[v] -= 1
            if in_degree[v] == 0:
                queue.append(v)

    # Compute grundy numbers in reverse topological order
    grundy = [0] * n
    for u in reversed(topo):
        s = set()
        for v in adj[u]:
            s.add(grundy[v])
        mex = 0
        while mex in s:
            mex += 1
        grundy[u] = mex

    total_xor = 0
    for g in grundy:
        total_xor ^= g

    if total_xor == 0:
        print("LOSE")
        return

    # Find a node v where total_xor ^ grundy[v] ^ xor_out == 0
    for v in range(n):
        xor_out = 0
        for u in adj[v]:
            xor_out ^= grundy[u]
        if (total_xor ^ grundy[v] ^ xor_out) == 0:
            # Set v and its out-neighbors to 0
            new_h = h.copy()
            new_h[v] = 0
            for u in adj[v]:
                new_h[u] = 0
            print("WIN")
            print(' '.join(map(str, new_h)))
            return

    print("LOSE")

if __name__ == "__main__":

   while there are parentheses in s:
      find all innermost parentheses
      for each pair (st, ed), get inner substring
         replace the substring with expand(expand_inner(s[st+1:ed]))

      identify sequences forming trains (tokens sequences ending with function, length at least 2)
      for each such train sequence:
          determine if it's part of a monadic or dyadic application
          expand it according to the rules
   return the resulting string

class FenwickTree:
    def __init__(self, size):
        self.n = size
        self.tree = [0] * (self.n + 2)

    def update(self, index, delta):
        while index <= self.n:
            self.tree[index] += delta
            index += index & -index

    def query(self, index):
        res = 0
        while index > 0:
            res += self.tree[index]
            index -= index & -index
        return res

n = int(input())
s = list(map(int, input().split()))
fenwick = FenwickTree(n)
for x in range(1, n+1):
    fenwick.update(x, x)

result = []
for i in range(n-1, -1, -1):
    current_s = s[i]
    low = 1
    high = n
    ans = -1
    while low <= high:
        mid = (low + high) // 2
        sum_mid_minus_1 = fenwick.query(mid - 1)
        if sum_mid_minus_1 < current_s:
            low = mid + 1
        elif sum_mid_minus_1 > current_s:
            high = mid - 1
        else:
            sum_mid = fenwick.query(mid)
            if sum_mid - sum_mid_minus_1 == mid:
                ans = mid
                break
            else:
                low = mid + 1
    result.append(ans)
    fenwick.update(ans, -ans)

result.reverse()
print(' '.join(map(str, result)))<#sep#> I can write 4^{ log_3 2 } as e^{ ln(4) * log_3 2 } and see if that can be expressed in terms of log_2 or something?

n = int(input())
p = list(map(int, input().split()))

pos = [0] * (n + 1)  # 1-based indexing for values
for i in range(n):
    val = p[i]
    pos[val] = i  # 0-based index

class FenwickTree:
    def __init__(self, size):
        self.n = size
        self.tree = [0] * (self.n + 2)  # 1-based indexing

    def update(self, idx):
        while idx <= self.n:
            self.tree[idx] += 1
            idx += idx & -idx

    def query(self, idx):
        res = 0
        while idx > 0:
            res += self.tree[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        return res

    def get_count_greater(self, x):
        return self.query(self.n) - self.query(x + 1)

ft = FenwickTree(n)
min_pos = None
max_pos = None
inversion_count = 0
result = []

for k in range(1, n + 1):
    current_pos = pos[k]
    count = ft.get_count_greater(current_pos)
    inversion_count += count

    if k == 1:
        min_pos = max_pos = current_pos
    else:
        if current_pos < min_pos:
            min_pos = current_pos
        if current_pos > max_pos:
            max_pos = current_pos

    term = (max_pos - min_pos + 1) - k
    res = inversion_count + term
    result.append(str(res))

    ft.update(current_pos + 1)  # Convert 0-based to 1-based index

print(' '.join(result))<#sep#> 4}, which is correct.

assistant: **Problem Statement:**
1. The `Btn` class has a default `msg` value of "Click" which should be "" (empty) for icon-only buttons
2. The icon alignment logic doesn't automatically center icons when there's no text

**Problem Location:**
- File: `/testbed/sepal_ui/sepalwidgets/btn.py`
- Lines 39 (default msg), 58 (icon initialization), 86-94 (_set_text method)

**How the test reproduces the issue:**
- `test_init` fails because `btn.children[1]` returns "Click" instead of ""
- `test_set_gliph` fails because `btn.v_icon.left` remains `True` instead of becoming `False` when `msg=""`

**Best practices for the fix:**
- Maintain backward compatibility
- Automatically handle icon alignment based on text content
- Make minimal changes to the existing API

**How to fix the problem:**
1. Change default `msg` parameter from "Click" to ""
2. Add logic in `_set_text` method to automatically set `v_icon.left = False` when `msg` is empty
3. Add logic to set `v_icon.left = True` when `msg` has content
4. Handle special case in constructor when `icon=True` is passed

n, k = map(int, input().split())

type10 = []
type01 = []
type11 = []

for _ in range(n):
    t, a, b = map(int, input().split())
    if a == 1 and b == 1:
        type11.append(t)
    elif a == 1 and b == 0:
        type10.append(t)
    elif a == 0 and b == 1:
        type01.append(t)

# Sort each type and compute prefix sums
type10.sort()
type01.sort()
type11.sort()

prefix10 = [0]
for t in type10:
    prefix10.append(prefix10[-1] + t)

prefix01 = [0]
for t in type01:
    prefix01.append(prefix01[-1] + t)

prefix11 = [0]
for t in type11:
    prefix11.append(prefix11[-1] + t)

m = len(type11)
len10 = len(type10)
len01 = len(type01)

min_total = float('inf')
max_s = min(m, k)

for s in range(0, max_s + 1):
    required_a = k - s
    required_b = k - s
    if required_a <= len10 and required_b <= len01:
        total = prefix11[s] + prefix10[required_a] + prefix01[required_b]
        if total < min_total:
            min_total = total

# Check if we can take s >=k
if m >= k:
    total = prefix11[k]
    if total < min_total:
        min_total = total

print(-1 if min_total == float('inf') else min_total)<#sep#>):

**Final Answer**
\boxed{2^{\log_3 4} - \sqrt{5}}
</think>To find \( f(2) - g(2) \) for the given functions \( f(x) = (x+2)^{\frac{\lg x}{\lg (x+1)}} \) and \( g(x) = x^{\frac{\lg (x+3)}{\lg (x+2)}} \), we start by evaluating each function at \( x = 2 \).

First, we evaluate \( f(2) \):
- The base of \( f(2) \) is \( 2 + 2 = 4 \).
- The exponent is \( \frac{\lg 2}{\lg 3} \), which is equivalent to \( \log_3 2 \).
- Therefore, \( f(2) = 4^{\log_3 2} \).

Next, we evaluate \( g(2) \):
- The base of \( g(2) \) is \( 2 \).
- The exponent is \( \frac{\lg 5}{\lg 4} \), which is equivalent to \( \log_4 5 \).
- Using the identity \( a^{\log_b c} = c^{\log_b a} \), we rewrite \( 2^{\log_4 5} \) as \( 5^{\log_4 2} \).
- Since \( \log_4 2 = \frac{1}{2} \), this simplifies to \( 5^{1/2} = \sqrt{5} \).
- Therefore, \( g(2) = \sqrt{5} \).

Finally, we compute the difference \( f(2) - g(2) \):
- \( f(2) = 4^{\log_3 2} \)
- \( g(2) = \sqrt{5} \)

We can rewrite \( 4^{\log_3 2} \) as \( (2^2)^{\log_3 2} = 2^{2 \log_3 2} \), and using the property \( 2 \log_3 2 = \log_3 4 \), we have \( 2^{2 \log_3 2} = 2^{\log_3 4} \).

Thus, the value of \( f(2) - g(2) \) is:
\[
2^{\log_3 4} - \sqrt{5}
\]

Therefore, the final answer is:
\[
\boxed{2^{\log_3 4} - \sqrt{5}}
\]<#sep#>ic 极 the \n mad1 case.

In JIS X 0208 a 94 by 94 map (kuten (区点)) is defined to encode Chinese characters (kanji), phonetic moras (kana) and other symbols. Along with this standard there are 3 different encodings that maintain 1-1 correspondences with the map, namely JIS, Shift_JIS and EUC. These encodings take 2 bytes for each character in the kuten map. The mappings are different that random characters may occur (mojibake) if the wrong encoding is used.
Conversion Algorithm
In the formulae below, kuten is an ordered pair of numbers \$(区,点)\$ where \$1\le 区,点\le94\$, and \$(a,b)\$ is an ordered pair of bytes where \$0\le a,b\le 255\$. The conversions between kuten and the 3 encodings are as follows:
$$\text{JIS}: (a,b)=(32+区,32+点)$$
$$\text{Shift_JIS}: (a,b)=\begin{cases}
\left(128+\left\lceil\frac{区}{2}\right\rceil+64\left\lfloor\frac{区}{63}\right\rfloor,63+点+\left\lfloor\frac{点}{64}\right\rfloor\right)&\text{if }区\text{ is odd}\\
\left(128+\left\lceil\frac{区}{2}\right\rceil+64\left\lfloor\frac{区}{63}\right\rfloor,158+点\right)&\text{if }区\text{ is even}
\end{cases}$$
$$\text{EUC}: (a,b)=(160+区,160+点)$$
Challenge
Write a program or function that, given a byte pair, the source encoding and the destination encoding, converts the byte pair from the source encoding to the destination encoding and outputs the result. You may assume that the input byte pair represents a valid codepoint in the source encoding.
The input and output formats are flexible and are accepted if reasonable. For example, you may receive or output the byte pair as a list, as two numbers or as a number, either in decimal or hexadecimal; you may also assign a number to each of the possible encodings and use them as the input format of the encodings.
Sample IO
The format used in the samples is SrcCodePoint, SrcEncoding, DstEncoding -> DstCodePoint. Decimal is used here but hexadecimal is also acceptable. The Unicode character and its assigned kuten are for explanation.
(227, 129), Shift_JIS, EUC -> (229, 225) (Kuten: 69-65; U+7E3A 縺)
(101, 98), JIS, Shift_JIS  -> (227, 130) (Kuten: 69-66; U+7E67 繧)
(229, 227), EUC, JIS       -> (101, 99)  (Kuten: 69-67; U+7E5D 繝)
(137, 126), Shift_JIS, JIS -> (49, 95)   (Kuten: 17-63; U+5186 円)
(177, 224), EUC, Shift_JIS -> (137, 128) (Kuten: 17-64; U+5712 園)
(49, 97), JIS, EUC         -> (177, 225) (Kuten: 17-65; U+5830 堰)

See here (in Japanese) for the full table.
Winning condition
This is a code-golf challenge, so the shortest valid submission for each language wins. Standard loopholes are forbidden by default.

MOD = 10**9 + 7
m_max = 200000

# Precompute the DP table
dp = [[0] * (m_max + 1) for _ in range(10)]
for d in range(10):
    dp[d][0] = 1  # Base case: 0 operations

for k in range(1, m_max + 1):
    for d in range(10):
        if d + 1 < 10:
            dp[d][k] = dp[d+1][k-1] % MOD
        else:
            dp[d][k] = (dp[0][k-1] + dp[1][k-1]) % MOD

import math

a, b, c = map(int, input().split())

if a == 0:
    if b == 0:
        if c == 0:
            print(-1)
        else:
            print(0)
    else:
        x = -c / b
        print(1)
        print("{0:.10f}".format(x))
else:
    discriminant = b**2 - 4 * a * c
    if discriminant < 0:
        print(0)
    elif discriminant == 0:
        x = (-b) / (2 * a)
        print(1)
        print("{0:.10f}".format(x))
    else:
        sqrt_d = math.sqrt(discriminant)
        x1 = (-b - sqrt_d) / (2 * a)
        x2 = (-b + sqrt_d) / (2 * a)
        roots = sorted([x1, x2])

S = ∑_{n=2}^\infty (-1)^n / (n log n).

Express 1/log n as ∫_{0}^{\infty} n^{-t} dt. Then,

Assuming we can interchange sum and integral, which I think requires uniform convergence. Let's check for convergence of the sum:

For each fixed t ≥ 0, the sum ∑_{n=2}^\infty (-1)^n / n^{t + 1} is an alternating series. For t ≥ 0, the terms 1/n^{t + 1} decrease to zero. Thus, by the Alternating Series Test, the series converges for each t ≥ 0. Moreover, the convergence is uniform on [0, ∞)? Not sure. So, S = ∫_{0}^{\infty} [ -η(t + 1) + 1 ] dt, as earlier. But η(t + 1) = ∑_{n=1}^\infty (-1)^{n-1} / n^{t + 1} = ∑_{n=1}^\infty (-1)^{n-1} / n^{s} where s = t + 1.

Wait, the Dirichlet eta function is related to the Riemann zeta function: η(s) = (1 - 2^{1 - s}) ζ(s). So, η(t + 1) = (1 - 2^{-t}) ζ(t + 1). Then,

S = ∫_{0}^{\infty} [1 - (1 - 2^{-t}) ζ(t + 1) ] dt.

But ζ(t + 1) for t > 0 is analytic except at t + 1 = 1, i.e., t = 0. But ζ(s) has a pole at s=1. So as t approaches 0 from the right, ζ(t + 1) ~ 1/(t) + γ + ... So the integrand near t=0 is 1 - (1 - 1) ζ(1) but 2^{-t} as t approaches 0 is 1 - t log 2 + ... So expanding the integrand near t=0:

1 - (1 - 2^{-t}) ζ(t + 1) ≈ 1 - [1 - (1 - t log 2 + ...)][1/t + γ + ...]

= 1 - [ (t log 2 + ... ) (1/t + γ + ... ) ]

= 1 - [ log 2 + γ t + ... ] = 1 - log 2 + O(t).

So the integrand approaches 1 - log 2 as t approaches 0. Wait, but when t approaches 0, ζ(t + 1) ~ 1/t + γ. So plugging in:

1 - (1 - 2^{-t}) ζ(t + 1) ≈ 1 - (t log 2) * (1/t + γ ) = 1 - log 2 - γ t log 2.

So as t approaches 0, the integrand approaches 1 - log 2. So there's a finite limit at t=0. As t approaches infinity, ζ(t + 1) approaches 1, and 2^{-t} approaches 0. Therefore, the integrand becomes 1 - (1 - 0)*1 = 0. So the integrand starts at 1 - log 2 and approaches 0 as t goes to infinity. Therefore, the integral might converge.

from collections import defaultdict

s = input().strip()
counts = defaultdict(int)
month_days = [31, 28, 31, 30, 31, 30, 31, 31, 30, 31, 30, 31]

for i in range(len(s) - 9):
    substring = s[i:i+10]
    if substring[2] != '-' or substring[5] != '-':
        continue
    day_part = substring[:2]
    month_part = substring[3:5]
    year_part = substring[6:10]
    if not (day_part.isdigit() and month_part.isdigit() and year_part.isdigit()):
        continue
    day = int(day_part)
    month = int(month_part)
    year = int(year_part)
    if year < 2013 or year > 2015:
        continue
    if month < 1 or month > 12:
        continue
    max_day = month_days[month - 1]
    if day < 1 or day > max_day:
        continue
    counts[substring] += 1

max_count = -1
result = ""
for date, cnt in counts.items():
    if cnt > max_count:
        max_count = cnt
        result = date

def main():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.read().splitlines()
    n, r, c = map(int, input[0].split())
    words = input[1].split()
    if not words:
        print()
        return

    # Precompute prefix sums and s array
    prefix_sum = [0] * (n + 1)
    for i in range(n):
        prefix_sum[i + 1] = prefix_sum[i] + len(words[i])
    s = [prefix_sum[i] + i for i in range(n + 1)]

    # Compute next_line_start
    next_line_start = [0] * n
    for l in range(n):
        target = s[l] + c + 1
        low, high, ans = l, n, l
        while low <= high:
            mid = (low + high) // 2
            if s[mid] <= target:
                ans = mid
                low = mid + 1
            else:
                high = mid - 1
        next_line_start[l] = ans

    # Build binary lifting table
    max_k = 20
    jump = [[n] * (n + 1) for _ in range(max_k)]
    for i in range(n):
        jump[0][i] = next_line_start[i]
    jump[0][n] = n

    for k in range(1, max_k):
        for i in range(n + 1):
            if jump[k - 1][i] <= n:
                jump[k][i] = jump[k - 1][jump[k - 1][i]]
            else:
                jump[k][i] = n

    # Find the best_i and best_end
    max_length = 0
    best_i = 0
    best_end = 0
    for i in range(n):
        current = i
        remaining = r
        for k in reversed(range(max_k)):
            if remaining >= (1 << k):
                next_current = jump[k][current]
                if next_current <= n:
                    current = next_current
                    remaining -= (1 << k)
        end = current
        if end - i > max_length:
            max_length = end - i
            best_i = i
            best_end = end

    # Generate the output lines
    current = best_i
    lines = []
    while current < best_end and len(lines) < r:
        next_line = next_line_start[current]
        line_words = words[current:next_line]
        lines.append(' '.join(line_words))
        current = next_line

    # Print up to r lines
    for line in lines[:r]:
        print(line)

if __name__ == '__main__':

def is_lucky(s):
    return all(c in {'0', '4', '7'} for c in s)

def solve():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.read().split()
    t = int(input[0])
    cases = input[1:t+1]

    # Precompute possible sums for 6 digits of 0,4,7
    max_sum = 6*7
    possible = [False] * (max_sum + 1)
    # Generate all possible sums with exactly 6 digits
    from itertools import product
    for digits in product([0,4,7], repeat=6):
        s = sum(digits)
        possible[s] = True

    for n_str in cases:
        n = int(n_str)
        digits = list(map(int, reversed(n_str)))
        length = len(digits)
        max_len = 18
        digits = digits + [0]*(max_len - length)
        
        # DP[i][carry]: whether it's possible to process up to i-th digit with carry
        dp = [ [ -1 for _ in range(5) ] for _ in range(max_len+1) ]
        dp[0][0] = 0  # starting with 0 carry at position 0
        for i in range(max_len):
            current_digit = digits[i]
            for carry_in in range(5):
                if dp[i][carry_in] == -1:
                    continue
                for carry_out in range(5):
                    sum_digits = current_digit + 10 * carry_out - carry_in
                    if sum_digits < 0 or sum_digits > max_sum:
                        continue
                    if possible[sum_digits]:
                        dp[i+1][carry_out] = carry_in
                        break
        
        if dp[max_len][0] == -1:
            print(-1)
            continue
        
        # Backtrack to find the digits for each of the six numbers
        nums = [[] for _ in range(6)]
        carry = 0
        for i in range(max_len):
            current_digit = digits[i]
            # Find the carry_out that was used
            for carry_out in range(5):
                if dp[i+1][car_out] == carry:
                    sum_digits = current_digit + 10 * carry_out - carry
                    break
            # Now find a combination of 6 digits that sum to sum_digits
            # This can be slow, but we can generate one valid combination
            found = False
            for d0 in [0,4,7]:
                for d1 in [0,4,7]:
                    for d2 in [0,4,7]:
                        for d3 in [0,4,7]:
                            for d4 in [0,4,7]:
                                d5 = sum_digits - (d0+d1+d2+d3+d4)
                                if d5 in {0,4,7} and d5 >=0:
                                    nums[0].append(d0)
                                    nums[1].append(d1)
                                    nums[2].append(d2)
                                    nums[3].append(d3)
                                    nums[4].append(d4)
                                    nums[5].append(d5)
                                    found = True
                                    break
                            if found:
                                break
                        if found:
                            break
                    if found:
                        break
                if found:
                    break
            # Update carry for next digit
            carry = carry_out
        
        # Construct the numbers
        res = []
        for num_digits in nums:
            # Reverse the digits to get the correct order
            s = ''.join(map(str, reversed(num_digits))).lstrip('0')
            if not s:
                s = '0'
            res.append(s)
        
        # Check if sum is correct
        total = sum(int(x) for x in res)
        if total != n:
            print(-1)
        else:
            print(' '.join(res))

def convert_to_base(n, k):
    if n == 0:
        return '0'
    digits = []
    while n > 0:
        digits.append(str(n % k))
        n = n // k
    return ''.join(reversed(digits))

k = int(input())

for i in range(1, k):
    row = []
    for j in range(1, k):
        product = i * j
        row.append(convert_to_base(product, k))
    print(' '.join(row))<#sep#>0.0933

</think>To find the sum of the series \(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n+1}}{(n+1)\log(n+1)}\), we will first verify its convergence using the Alternating Series Test and then discuss why the sum does not have a closed-form expression.

### Step 1: Verify Convergence Using the Alternating Series Test

The series is given by:
\[
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty} \frac{(-1)^{n+1}}{(n+1)\log(n+1)}
\]

To apply the Alternating Series Test, we need to check two conditions for the sequence \(a_n = \frac{1}{(n+1)\log(n+1)}\):

1. **Monotonicity**: The sequence \(a_n\) must be decreasing.
2. **Limit**: The limit of \(a_n\) as \(n\) approaches infinity must be zero.

#### Monotonicity

Consider the function \(f(x) = \frac{1}{(x+1)\log(x+1)}\). We need to show that \(f(x)\) is decreasing for \(x \geq 1\). Taking the derivative of \(f(x)\):

\[
f'(x) = \frac{d}{dx} \left( \frac{1}{(x+1)\log(x+1)} \right)
\]

Using the quotient rule:

\[
f'(x) = \frac{-(\log(x+1) + 1)}{(x+1)^2 (\log(x+1))^2}
\]

Since \(\log(x+1) + 1 > 0\) for \(x \geq 1\), \(f'(x) < 0\), which implies that \(f(x)\) is decreasing. Therefore, \(a_n\) is decreasing.

#### Limit

\[
\lim_{n \to \infty} \frac{1}{(n+1)\log(n+1)} = 0
\]

Both conditions of the Alternating Series Test are satisfied, so the series converges.

### Step 2: Sum of the Series

To find the exact sum of the series, we explore various approaches, including integral representations and generating functions. However, these approaches do not lead to a closed-form expression.

#### Integral Representation

Consider the integral representation of \(\frac{1}{\log(n+1)}\):

\[
\frac{1}{\log(n+1)} = \int_{0}^{\infty} (n+1)^{-t} \, dt
\]

Substituting this into the series:

\[
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty} \frac{(-1)^{n+1}}{(n+1)\log(n+1)} = \sum_{n=1}^{\infty} \frac{(-1)^{n+1}}{n+1} \int_{0}^{\infty} (n+1)^{-t} \, dt
\]

Interchanging the sum and the integral (justified by uniform convergence):

\[
\int_{0}^{\infty} \sum_{n=1}^{\infty} \frac{(-1)^{n+1}}{(n+1)^{t+1}} \, dt
\]

The inner sum is related to the Dirichlet eta function \(\eta(t+1)\), but this does not simplify to a known closed-form expression.

#### Generating Functions

Consider the generating function \(f(x) = \sum_{n=1}^{\infty} \frac{(-1)^{n+1} x^n}{(n+1)\log(n+1)}\). Differentiating or integrating \(f(x)\) does not simplify the series.

### Conclusion

After exploring various methods, it is concluded that the series does not have a closed-form expression in terms of elementary functions or well-known special functions. Therefore, the sum of the series is:

\[
\boxed{\text{The series converges but does not have a closed-form expression.}}

assistant: Perfect! I can clearly see the issues:

1. **Default text issue**: When creating `sw.Btn(icon=True, gliph="mdi-plus")`, it automatically adds "Click" text
2. **Icon alignment issue**: Even when `msg=""`, the icon still has `left=True` instead of being centered

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS

### 5.1 Problem Statement
The `sepal_ui.Btn` component has two main issues:
1. It defaults to "Click" text when no `msg` parameter is provided
2. When `msg` is empty, the icon remains left-aligned instead of being centered

### 5.2 Problem Location
The issues are in `/testbed/sepal_ui/sepalwidgets/btn.py`:
- Line 39: Default `msg="Click"` parameter
- Line 58: Icon always created with `left=True`
- Lines 86-94: `_set_text` method doesn't handle icon alignment

### 5.3 Test Reproduction
My reproduction script confirms both issues:
- Default text appears when not specified
- Icon alignment stays `left=True` even with empty message

### 5.4 Best Practices
- Maintain backward compatibility
- Handle icon alignment based on content presence
- Keep API simple and intuitive

### 5.5 Fix Approach
1. **Default text**: Change default to empty string `""` instead of `"Click"`
2. **Icon alignment**: Modify `_set_text` method to set `v_icon.left = False` when message is empty
3. **Icon alignment**: Set `v_icon.left = True` when message has content

## Phase 6. FIX IMPLEMENTATION

n = int(input())
points = set()
x_to_ys = {}

for _ in range(n):
    x, y = map(int, input().split())
    points.add((x, y))
    if x not in x_to_ys:
        x_to_ys[x] = []
    x_to_ys[x].append(y)

count = 0

for x in x_to_ys:
    ys = sorted(x_to_ys[x])
    m = len(ys)
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(i + 1, m):
            d = ys[j] - ys[i]
            x_new = x + d
            if (x_new, ys[i]) in points and (x_new, ys[j]) in points:
                count += 1

def JIS_to_u(a,b): return (a-32, b-32)

def EUC_to_u(a,b): return (a-160, b-160)

def Shift_to_u(a,b):

    can = []

    if 129<=a<=159:

        k0=a-128

        e=2*k0

        if 1<= e <=62: can.append( (e,True) )

        o=e-1

        if 1<=o <=62: can.append( (o,False) )

    elif 224<=a<=239:

        bsa=a-192

        e=2*bsa

        if 63<=e <=94: can.append( (e,True) )

        o=e-1

        if 63<=o <=94: can.append( (o,False) )

    # process candidates:

    for (k, e) in can:

        if e:

            pt=b-158

            if 1<=pt<=94: return (k, pt)

        else:

            if b<=126: pt = b-63

            else: pt =b-64

            if 1<=pt<=94 and (63 + pt + (pt//64)) ==b:

                return (k, pt)

    return None #这个 shouldn't be reached as input are valid.

The codings for Encode functions:

def JIS_encode(ku,ten):

    return (32+ku, 32+ten)

def EUC_encode(ku, ten):

    return (160+ku, 160+ten)

def Shift_encode(ku, ten):

    is_odd = ku%2 !=0

    ceil_half = (ku+1)//2

    adj64 =64*(ku//63)

    a =128 + ceil_half + adj64

    # b:

    if is_odd:

        pt=ten

        b=63+pt + (pt//64)

    else:

        b=158+ ten

    return (a, b)

아然后, the overall functions:

def convert( from_enc, to_enc, a,b):

    if from_enc ==0: #JIS:

        ku, ten= JIS_to_u(a,b)

    elif from_enc==1: #ShiftJIS:

        ku,ten = Shift_to_u(a,b)

    elif from_enc ==2: #EUC:

        ku,ten=EUC_to_u(a,b)

    # then encode:

    if to_enc ==0:

        return JIS_encode(ku, ten)

    elif to_enc ==1:

        return Shift_encode(ku, ten)

    else:

        return EUC_encode(ku, ten)

= 1 + 9ω² + ω^4 + 6ω + 2ω² + 6ω³

Again, simplify higher powers:

7 + 7ω + 11ω² = 7(1 + ω + ω²) + 4ω² = 0 + 4ω² = 4ω²

Because 7*(1 + ω + ω²) is 0, so remaining is 4ω².

So, (1 + 3ω + ω²)^2 = 4ω².

So now, if we take the two expressions: (1 + ω + 3ω²)^2 = 4ω, and (1 + 3ω + ω²)^2 = 4ω². Adding these two gives 4ω + 4ω² = 4(ω + ω²). But ω + ω² = -1 (since 1 + ω + ω² = 0), so 4*(-1) = -4. Therefore, the sum of these two expressions is -4.

Therefore, the original expression (1 + ω + 3ω²)^2 = 4ω is part of the sum (with the other being 4ω²) that adds up to -4.

So that's probably what the problem is asking for. Hence, when 4ω is added to 4ω², the sum is -4. Therefore, the value of the given expression is part of such a sum.

Alternatively, maybe the problem is referring to a sum that includes the original expression and another expression involving ω and ω², but I think this is the most plausible approach.

Therefore, the answer is that when (1 + ω + 3ω²)^2 = 4ω is added to (1 + 3ω + ω²)^2 = 4ω², the sum is 4ω + 4ω² = -4. Hence, the expression is part of the sum that equals -4.
</think>To simplify the expression \((1 + \omega + 3\omega^2)^2\) and show that its value is part of a sum that equals \(-4\) when added to another expression, we proceed as follows:

1. **Simplify the Expression \((1 + \omega + 3\omega^2)^2\):**

   Recall that \(\omega\) is a complex cube root of unity, so \(\omega^3 = 1\) and \(1 + \omega + \omega^2 = 0\). Using these properties, we can simplify the expression step-by-step.

   First, expand \((1 + \omega + 3\omega^2)^2\):

   \[
   (1 + \omega + 3\omega^2)^2 = 1^2 + \omega^2 + (3\omega^2)^2 + 2(1 \cdot \omega + 1 \cdot 3\omega^2 + \omega \cdot 3\omega^2)
   \]

   Simplify each term:

   \[
   = 1 + \omega^2 + 9\omega^4 + 2\omega + 6\omega^2 + 6\omega^3
   \]

   Using \(\omega^3 = 1\) and \(\omega^4 = \omega\):

   \[
   = 1 + \omega^2 + 9\omega + 2\omega + 6\omega^2 + 6 \cdot 1
   \]

   Combine like terms:

   \[
   = 1 + 6 + \omega^2 + 6\omega^2 + 9\omega + 2\omega
   \]

   \[
   = 7 + 7\omega^2 + 11\omega
   \]

   We can use the identity \(1 + \omega + \omega^2 = 0\) to simplify further. Notice that:

   \[
   7 + 7\omega^2 + 11\omega = 7(1 + \omega + \omega^2) + 4\omega = 7 \cdot 0 + 4\omega = 4\omega
   \]

   Therefore, the simplified expression is:

   \[
   (1 + \omega + 3\omega^2)^2 = 4\omega
   \]

2. **Find the Other Expression:**

   To show that the value of the expression is part of a sum that equals \(-4\) when added to another expression, consider the expression \((1 + 3\omega + \omega^2)^2\):

   \[
   (1 + 3\omega + \omega^2)^2
   \]

   Expand this expression similarly:

   \[
   (1 + 3\omega + \omega^2)^2 = 1^2 + (3\omega)^2 + (\omega^2)^2 + 2(1 \cdot 3\omega + 1 \cdot \omega^2 + 3\omega \cdot \omega^2)
   \]

   Simplify each term:

   \[
   = 1 + 9\omega^2 + \omega^4 + 6\omega + 2\omega^2 + 6\omega^3
   \]

   Using \(\omega^3 = 1\) and \(\omega^4 = \omega\):

   \[
   = 1 + 9\omega^2 + \omega + 6\omega + 2\omega^2 + 6 \cdot 1
   \]

   Combine like terms:

   \[
   = 1 + 6 + 9\omega^2 + 2\omega^2 + 6\omega + \omega
   \]

   \[
   = 7 + 11\omega^2 + 7\omega
   \]

   Again, using the identity \(1 + \omega + \omega^2 = 0\):

   \[
   7 + 11\omega^2 + 7\omega = 7(1 + \omega + \omega^2) + 4\omega^2 = 7 \cdot 0 + 4\omega^2 = 4\omega^2
   \]

   Therefore, the simplified expression is:

   \[
   (1 + 3\omega + \omega^2)^2 = 4\omega^2
   \]

3. **Sum the Two Expressions:**

   Now, add the two simplified expressions:

   \[
   4\omega + 4\omega^2 = 4(\omega + \omega^2)
   \]

   Using the identity \(1 + \omega + \omega^2 = 0\), we have \(\omega + \omega^2 = -1\):

   \[
   4(\omega + \omega^2) = 4(-1) = -4
   \]

Therefore, the value of the given expression \((1 + \omega + 3\omega^2)^2 = 4\omega\) is part of the sum that equals \(-4\) when added to \((1 + 3\omega + \omega^2)^2 = 4\omega^2\). The final answer is:

\[
\boxed{4\omega}
\]<#sep#>human: Princess Tetra of the Kingdom of Palace is known as an unrivaled puzzle lover, and has recently been enthusiastic about her own "Tetra puzzle". This is a puzzle that uses a board with equilateral triangle squares and a regular tetrapod block consisting of four equilateral triangle panels called a tetrapod. The purpose of the puzzle is to develop all the tetrapods on the board so as to meet the following conditions.


* The expanded tetrapod covers the square on which it was placed and two different squares specified for each.
* None of the squares are covered by more than one panel



Figure F-1 shows an example of a Tetra puzzle problem and an example of its solution. Tetrapods are placed on the ★ and ● squares, and are specified to cover the ☆ and ○ squares, respectively. In the answer example, the squares with horizontal lines are the squares covered with the tetrapods placed in the squares with ★, and the squares with vertical lines are the squares covered with the tetrapods placed in the squares with ●.


<image>

Figure F-1: Tetra puzzle question example (left) and answer example (right)

Figure F-2 is an invalid answer example in the problem example in the above figure. In the example on the left, the square directly above the square of ★ is covered with two panels, and in the example on the right, each is not a shape obtained by expanding the tetrapod, so it is not accepted as an answer.

<image>

Figure F-2: Invalid answer example

Princess Tetra has created a collection of puzzle questions for the upcoming summer festival so that many people will know the fun of puzzles. And you, who are trusted by the princess as the second puzzle lover in the kingdom, have been appointed to check the problem collection.


By the way, as you proceeded with the check work, you realized that you were in trouble. It seems that the problem collection contains puzzles for which there is no solution. If you want to respect the puzzle made by the princess as much as possible, you can solve the puzzle by deleting one of the tetrapods together with the information of the square that the tetrapod should cover for the puzzle that does not have a solution. I decided to change it. There is only 3 hours left until the check deadline given by the princess. Let's review the problem collection and fix it quickly.
Input

The input consists of multiple datasets. The format of each data set is as follows.



n
x1a y1a x1b y1b x1c y1c
x2a y2a x2b y2b x2c y2c
...
xna yna xnb ynb xnc ync


n (2 ≤ n ≤ 5,000) is an integer representing the number of tetrapods placed on the board. Each tetrapod is assigned a number from 1 to n.


The next n lines consist of 6 integers, each separated by a space. (xia, yia) represents the coordinates of the cell in which the i-th tetrapod is located, and (xib, yib) and (xic, yic) are the coordinates of the cell to be covered when the i-th tetrapod is expanded, respectively. Represents coordinates. It can be assumed that the absolute values ​​of the given x and y coordinates are 20,000 or less. Also, it can be assumed that the given coordinates are all different from each other. That is, two or more tetrapods are not placed in the same square, the square in which the tetrapod is placed is not designated as the square to be covered, and the square to be covered is not specified in duplicate.


Coordinates are assigned to each cell as shown in Fig. F-3.


<image>

Figure F-3: Coordinates assigned to the square

n = 0 indicates the end of input. This is not included in the dataset.


Output

For each dataset, output Valid on one line if the puzzle can be solved without removing the tetrapod. If removing only one tetrapod makes it possible to solve the puzzle, output Remove on one line, and then removing it continuously outputs the number of the tetrapod that can solve the puzzle on one line in ascending order. do it. If the puzzle cannot be solved without removing two or more tetrapods, output Invalid on one line.

Sample Input


3
2 3 2 2 2 1
2 0 1 -1 2 -1
-2 -1 -1 -1 -2 0
6
-1 0 1 0 0 -1
-2 -1 -3 -2 -4 -2
2 -1 2 0 1 -1
4 -2 3 -2 3 -1
1 1 0 1 -1 1
-3 -1 -1 -1 -2 0
Five
-1 2 0 2 1 2
-3 1 -2 1 -2 2
-2 0 -2 -1 -3 0
1 -1 0 -1 -1 -1
1 0 2 0 2 -1
Four
-2 0 -2 -1 -3 -1
-1 -1 0 -1 1 -1
2 -1 2 0 3 -1
-1 0 0 0 1 0
Five
-2 1 3 -1 2 1
-5 2 -5 3 -4 2
-2 -1 0 -1 -3 -1
4 2 5 2 5 3
1 -1 2 -1 -1 -1
0

2b. Else, check for each i from 1 to n, if removing i allows a valid solution. Collect all such i's. If at least one exists, output "Remove" followed by the sorted list.

2c. Else, output "Invalid".

</think>To solve the equation \(20m + 18n = 2018\) for positive integers \(m\) and \(n\), we first note that the greatest common divisor (gcd) of 20 and 18 is 2. Since 2018 is divisible by 2, solutions exist. We can simplify the equation by dividing through by 2:

\[10m + 9n = 1009\]

Next, we solve for \(m\):

\[10m = 1009 - 9n\]
\[m = \frac{1009 - 9n}{10}\]

For \(m\) to be a positive integer, \(1009 - 9n\) must be divisible by 10. This implies:

\[1009 - 9n \equiv 0 \pmod{10}\]
\[9n \equiv 1009 \pmod{10}\]
\[9n \equiv 9 \pmod{10}\]

Since 9 and 10 are coprime, we can multiply both sides by the modular inverse of 9 modulo 10, which is 9 (since \(9 \times 9 \equiv 1 \pmod{10}\)):

\[n \equiv 1 \pmod{10}\]

Thus, \(n\) can be written as \(n = 10k + 1\) for non-negative integers \(k\). Substituting \(n = 10k + 1\) into the simplified equation:

\[10m + 9(10k + 1) = 1009\]
\[10m + 90k + 9 = 1009\]
\[10m = 1000 - 90k\]
\[m = 100 - 9k\]

For \(m\) to be positive, we need:

\[100 - 9k > 0\]
\[9k < 100\]
\[k < \frac{100}{9} \approx 11.11\]

Thus, \(k\) can take integer values from 0 to 11, inclusive, giving 12 possible values. Additionally, substituting \(k\) into \(n = 10k + 1\) ensures \(n\) remains positive for all valid \(k\).

For \( n = 1 \), let \( f(1) = 0 \). Find all pairs of integer polynomials \( P(x) \) and \( Q(x) \) such that for any positive integer \( m \), \( f(P(m)) = Q(f(m)) \) holds.

Okay, so I need to find all pairs of integer polynomials P(x) and Q(x) such that for any positive integer m, the function f(P(m)) equals Q(f(m)). Let me start by understanding the problem thoroughly.

First, let's recall what the function f(n) represents. For any positive integer n > 1, if n is factored into primes as p₁^α₁ p₂^α₂ … p_k^α_k, then f(n) is the sum of the exponents α₁ + α₂ + … + α_k. For n = 1, f(1) is 0. So essentially, f(n) counts the number of prime factors of n with multiplicity. For example, f(12) = f(2²*3¹) = 2 + 1 = 3.

Now, the problem asks for integer polynomials P(x) and Q(x) such that applying P to m and then taking f of that result is the same as applying Q to f(m). So for every positive integer m, f(P(m)) must equal Q(f(m)).

Let me consider possible forms of P and Q. Since both are integer polynomials, they have the form P(x) = a_n x^n + ... + a_0 and Q(x) = b_m x^m + ... + b_0 with integer coefficients.

First, let's think about simple cases. Maybe Q is a linear polynomial, or maybe something else. Let's try to find examples.

Case 1: P(x) is a constant polynomial. If P(x) = c, then f(P(m)) is f(c) for all m. Then Q(f(m)) must also be a constant. But Q is a polynomial, so if Q(f(m)) is constant for all m, then Q must be a constant polynomial. So Q(x) = d for some integer d, and f(c) = d. Therefore, c must be 1 or a prime number, because f(1) = 0, so if c=1, d=0. If c is a prime, then f(c) = 1, so d=1. So possible pairs here are P(x) = 1 and Q(x) = 0, or P(x) = p (a prime) and Q(x) = 1. But the problem says integer polynomials, so P(x) could be the constant polynomial 1 or any prime. However, primes are constants, but the coefficients are integers. Wait, primes are fixed integers, but if P(x) is a constant polynomial, then Q must also be a constant polynomial. However, the problem is to find all such pairs. But perhaps the only solutions are constant polynomials. But maybe there are non-constant P and Q. Let me think.

Case 2: Let's try P(x) = x. Then f(P(m)) = f(m) = Q(f(m)). So Q must be the identity polynomial, Q(x) = x. So (P(x) = x, Q(x) = x) is a solution.

Case 3: Let's check another simple polynomial. Suppose P(x) = x^k for some integer k ≥ 1. Then f(P(m)) = f(m^k). But m^k is m multiplied by itself k times. If m factors as a product of primes with exponents α_i, then m^k will have exponents k*α_i. So f(m^k) = k*α1 + k*α2 + ... + k*α_k = k*(α1 + α2 + ... + α_k) = k*f(m). Therefore, Q(f(m)) must equal k*f(m), so Q(x) = kx. Therefore, for P(x) = x^k, Q(x) = kx would work. For example, if P(x) = x², then Q(x) = 2x. So that seems to work. Let me verify with an example. Take m=6, which factors into 2*3, so f(6)=2. Then P(6)=6²=36=2²*3², so f(36)=2+2=4. Q(f(6))=Q(2)=2*2=4. It matches. Similarly, m=2, f(2)=1, P(2)=4, f(4)=2, Q(1)=2*1=2. Correct. So that works. But maybe there are other polynomials beyond monomials? Let's see.

class FenwickTree:
    def __init__(self, size):
        self.n = size
        self.tree = [0] * (self.n + 2)  # 1-based indexing

    def update(self, idx, delta):
        while idx <= self.n:
            self.tree[idx] += delta
            idx += idx & -idx

    def query(self, idx):
        res = 0
        while idx > 0:
            res += self.tree[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        return res

def main():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.read().split()
    ptr = 0
    n = int(input[ptr]); ptr +=1
    k = int(input[ptr]); ptr +=1
    a = int(input[ptr]); ptr +=1
    b = int(input[ptr]); ptr +=1
    q = int(input[ptr]); ptr +=1

    orders = [0] * (n + 2)  # days 1..n
    ft_b = FenwickTree(n)
    ft_a = FenwickTree(n)

    for _ in range(q):
        if input[ptr] == '1':
            di = int(input[ptr+1]); ptr +=2
            ai = int(input[ptr]); ptr +=1
            current = orders[di]
            new = current + ai
            # Update for b
            old_b = min(current, b)
            new_b = min(new, b)
            delta_b = new_b - old_b
            if delta_b != 0:
                ft_b.update(di, delta_b)
            # Update for a
            old_a = min(current, a)
            new_a = min(new, a)
            delta_a = new_a - old_a
            if delta_a != 0:
                ft_a.update(di, delta_a)
            orders[di] = new
        else:
            ptr +=1  # skip '2'
            pi = int(input[ptr]); ptr +=1
            sum_b = ft_b.query(pi -1)
            start_a = pi + k
            sum_a = ft_a.query(n) - ft_a.query(start_a -1)
            print(sum_b + sum_a)

tokens = input().split()
n = int(tokens[-1])
lhs_tokens = tokens[:-2]

coeff = [1]
for i in range(1, len(lhs_tokens), 2):
    op = lhs_tokens[i]
    coeff.append(1 if op == '+' else -1)

total_coeff = sum(coeff)
T = n - total_coeff

P = [i for i, c in enumerate(coeff) if c == 1]
N = [i for i, c in enumerate(coeff) if c == -1]

possible = False
if T == 0:
    possible = True
elif T > 0:
    if P and T <= len(P) * (n - 1):
        possible = True
else:
    required = -T
    if N and required <= len(N) * (n - 1):
        possible = True

if not possible:
    print("Impossible")

    if T > 0:
        sum_remaining = T
        for idx in P:
            if sum_remaining <= 0:
                break
            assign = min(sum_remaining, n - 1)
            x[idx] = assign
            sum_remaining -= assign
    elif T < 0:
        sum_remaining = -T
        for idx in N:
            if sum_remaining <= 0:
                break
            assign = min(sum_remaining, n - 1)
            x[idx] = assign
            sum_remaining -= assign

        if token == '?':

            term_idx += 1

            output.append(token)

If the tetrapod is required to form a shape with all four points including these two vectors, then the fourth point must be such that the four form congruent tetrapod structure.

Assuming that the vectors from H must sum to zero when combined with the fourth's vector.

Example:

Let me imagine that the fourth point C has vector HC, so that:

HA + HB + HC = 0.

Then HC would be the negative sum of the others.

In this case, HC = - (HA + HB). 

Hence coordinates of C are H plus ( (A.x - H.x) + (B.x-H.x), similar for Y).

So:

C.x = H.x + (A.x - H.x) + (B.x - H.x) 

= A.x + B.x - H.x 

Similarly,

C.y = A.y + B.y - H.y

So for the first sample Tet 1: 

H: (2,3)

A(2,2), B(2,1).

C.x = 2 + 2 -2 = 2 

C.y = 2 +1 -3 = 0 → (2,0).

Ah, but according to the problem's initial input constraints, the foot of the tet at (2,0) is indeed another's home, so since the initial input's condition says home and targets must be unique, the computed C (the inferred fourth square here) might be allowed as a fourth. Wait but the home is at (2,0) for tet2, which is forbidden to be part of any other's expansion.

Ah, right. So in this case, Tet1's fourth square would overlap with Tet2's home, making it impossible to place. Therefore this would be an overlap between Tet1's C and Tet2's home, thus requiring that tet1 is removed (as in the first sample's solution, it says removing tet1). 

So this approach seems to lead to plausible results, so perhaps this is the correct way to compute C.

Thus the formula is:

Fourth.x = A.x + B.x - H.x 

Fourth.y = A.y + B.y - H.y 

Then the four squares are H (home) plus A and B (the targets), and this Fourth.

import bisect
import math
from collections import defaultdict

def reverse_num(x):
    return int(str(x)[::-1].lstrip('0') or '0')

maxx, maxy, w = map(int, input().split())

# Preprocess a_ratios and b_ratios
a_ratios = defaultdict(list)
for a in range(1, maxx + 1):
    rev_a = reverse_num(a)
    d = math.gcd(a, rev_a)
    p = a // d
    q = rev_a // d
    a_ratios[(p, q)].append(a)

for key in a_ratios:
    a_ratios[key].sort()

b_ratios = defaultdict(list)
for b in range(1, maxy + 1):
    rev_b = reverse_num(b)
    d = math.gcd(rev_b, b)
    p = rev_b // d
    q = b // d
    b_ratios[(p, q)].append(b)

for key in b_ratios:
    b_ratios[key].sort()

common_ratios = set(a_ratios.keys()).intersection(b_ratios.keys())

min_product = float('inf')
best_x = -1
best_y = -1

for x in range(1, maxx + 1):
    current_a_counts = {}
    for (p, q) in common_ratios:
        a_list = a_ratios.get((p, q), [])
        cnt = bisect.bisect_right(a_list, x)
        current_a_counts[(p, q)] = cnt

    low, high = 1, maxy
    current_best_y = None
    while low <= high:
        mid = (low + high) // 2
        total = 0
        for (p, q) in common_ratios:
            a_cnt = current_a_counts.get((p, q), 0)
            if not a_cnt:
                continue
            b_list = b_ratios.get((p, q), [])
            b_cnt = bisect.bisect_right(b_list, mid)
            total += a_cnt * b_cnt
        if total >= w:
            current_best_y = mid
            high = mid - 1
        else:
            low = mid + 1
    if current_best_y is not None:
        product = x * current_best_y
        if product < min_product:
            min_product = product
            best_x, best_y = x, current_best_y

if best_x != -1:
    print(best_x, best_y)
else:
    print(-1)<#sep#>)=2*0 +2=2. Correct. m=2: P(2)=4*4=16, f(16)=4. Q(f(2))=Q(1)=2*1 +2=4. Correct. m=3: P(3)=4*9=36=2²*3², f(36)=4. Q(f(3))=Q(1)=4. Correct. m=4: P(4)=4*16=64=2^6, f(64)=6. Then the problem is to ensure that for all tet's:

- The fourth square is computed as the above formula.

- All four squares (home, targetA, targetB, fourth) are not overlapping with any other tet's home, target squares, or their computed fourth squares.

But since the initial input ensures that the home squares and targets are all unique, the only possible collision is between the computed fourth squares and other's homes, targets, or fourths OR between two different tets' computed fourth squares.

The steps for solving the problem become:

We need to collect all four squares of each tet: home, targetA, targetB, and the Fourth computed as mentioned.

Then check:

For all squares (including all four of each tet) are unique.

Additionally, for each tet, the Fourth returned must not conflict with the fourth of others, their own home, or targets (which are already taken care of by input constraints), but since the fourth could be a home or target of another tet.

So the full process steps for a dataset are as follows:

1. For each tet:

2. Collect all four points (h, a, b, fourth).

3. Check that all of these are unique across all the tet's Coverage.

- another tet's fourth with home.

- another tet's fourth with targets.

So the steps needed are:

For a given set S (the full set or missing one):

1. For each T in S:

   compute its fourth square.

   Collect all four (h, a, b, fourth) of T.

2. Check if all squares among all collected squares are unique.

   if Yes → OK.

Else → Not valid.

要使该问题成为Valid则原输入必须满足该条件。否则，当我们遍历去掉每个i后的集合（S\{i}）时，若其中一个i的集合满足条件，则可以保留i。

现在需要高效地实现这个。

Now, for the original data, we first test without any removal:

For each candidate removal (i):

- We create a new 计算集合 of all 四点的集合 for every tet except i.

- We check if this is valid (all unique?)

    17	    the color will be defaulted to 'primary' and can be changed afterward according to your need

    23	        icon (str, optional): the full name of any mdi/fa icon

    26	    .. deprecated:: 2.13
    27	        ``text`` and ``icon`` will be replaced by ``msg`` and ``gliph`` to avoid duplicating ipyvuetify trait.
    28	    """
    29	
    30	    v_icon = None
    31	    "v.Icon: the icon in the btn"
    32	
    33	    gliph = Unicode("").tag(sync=True)
    34	    "traitlet.Unicode: the name of the icon"
    35	
    36	    msg = Unicode("").tag(sync=True)
    37	    "traitlet.Unicode: the text of the btn"
    38	
    39	    def __init__(self, msg="Click", gliph="", **kwargs):
    40	
    41	        # deprecation in 2.13 of text and icon
    42	        # as they already exist in the ipyvuetify Btn traits (as booleans)
    43	        if "text" in kwargs:
    44	            if isinstance(kwargs["text"], str):
    45	                msg = kwargs.pop("text")
    46	                warnings.warn(
    47	                    '"text" is deprecated, please use "msg" instead', DeprecationWarning
    48	                )
    49	        if "icon" in kwargs:
    50	            if isinstance(kwargs["icon"], str):
    51	                gliph = kwargs.pop("icon")
    52	                warnings.warn(
    53	                    '"icon" is deprecated, please use "gliph" instead',
    54	                    DeprecationWarning,
    55	                )
    56	
    57	        # create the default v_icon
    58	        self.v_icon = v.Icon(left=True, children=[""])
    59	
    60	        # set the default parameters
    61	        kwargs["color"] = kwargs.pop("color", "primary")

    63	
    64	        # call the constructor
    65	        super().__init__(**kwargs)
    66	
    67	        self.gliph = gliph
    68	        self.msg = msg
    69	
    70	    @observe("gliph")
    71	    def _set_gliph(self, change):
    72	        """
    73	        Set a new icon. If the icon is set to "", then it's hidden
    74	        """
    75	        new_gliph = change["new"]
    76	        self.v_icon.children = [new_gliph]
    77	
    78	        # hide the component to avoid the right padding
    79	        if not new_gliph:
    80	            su.hide_component(self.v_icon)
    81	        else:
    82	            su.show_component(self.v_icon)
    83	
    84	        return self
    85	
    86	    @observe("msg")
    87	    def _set_text(self, change):
    88	        """
    89	        Set the text of the btn
    90	        """
    91	
    92	        self.children = [self.v_icon, change["new"]]
    93	
    94	        return self
    95	
    96	    @deprecated(version="2.14", reason="Replace by the private _set_gliph")

    99	        set a new icon. If the icon is set to "", then it's hidden.
   100	
   101	        Args:
   102	            icon (str, optional): the full name of a mdi/fa icon
   103	
   104	        Return:
   105	            self
   106	        """
   107	        self.gliph = icon
   108	        return self
   109	
   110	    def toggle_loading(self):
   111	        """
   112	        Jump between two states : disabled and loading - enabled and not loading
   113	
   114	        Return:
   115	            self
   116	        """
   117	        self.loading = not self.loading
   118	        self.disabled = self.loading
   119	
   120	        return self

   123	class DownloadBtn(v.Btn, SepalWidget):
   124	    """
   125	    Custom download Btn filled with the provided text.
   126	    the download icon is automatically embeded and green.
   127	    The btn only accepts absolute links. if non is provided then the btn stays disabled
   128	
   129	    Args:
   130	        text (str): the message inside the btn
   131	        path (str|pathlib.Path, optional): the absoluteor relative path to a downloadable content
   132	        args (dict, optional): any parameter from a v.Btn. if set, 'children' and 'target' will be overwritten.
   133	    """
   134	
   135	    def __init__(self, text, path="#", **kwargs):
   136	
   137	        # create a download icon
   138	        v_icon = v.Icon(left=True, children=["fas fa-download"])
   139	
   140	        # set default parameters
   141	        kwargs["class_"] = kwargs.pop("class_", "ma-2")
   142	        kwargs["xs5"] = kwargs.pop("xs5", True)
   143	        kwargs["color"] = kwargs.pop("color", "success")
   144	        kwargs["children"] = [v_icon, text]
   145	        kwargs["target"] = "_blank"
   146	        kwargs["attributes"] = {"download": None}
   147	
   148	        # call the constructor
   149	        super().__init__(**kwargs)
   150	
   151	        # create the URL
   152	        self.set_url(path)
   153	
   154	    def set_url(self, path="#"):
   155	        """
   156	        Set the URL of the download btn. and unable it.
   157	        If nothing is provided the btn is disabled
   158	
   159	        Args:
   160	            path (str|pathlib.Path): the absolute path to a downloadable content
   161	
   162	        Return:
   163	            self
   164	        """
   165	
   166	        # set the url
   167	        url = su.create_download_link(path)
   168	        self.href = url
   169	
   170	        # unable or disable the btn
   171	        self.disabled = str(path) == "#"
   172	
   173	        # set the download attribute
   174	        name = None if str(path) == "#" else Path(path).name
   175	        self.attributes = {"download": name}
   176	
   177	        return self

n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))
b = list(map(int, input().split()))
total_a = sum(a)

# Determine minimal k using sorted b in descending order
sorted_b = sorted(b, reverse=True)
prefix_sum = [0] * (n + 1)
for i in range(n):
    prefix_sum[i + 1] = prefix_sum[i] + sorted_b[i]
k = next(i for i in range(1, n + 1) if prefix_sum[i] >= total_a)

# Dynamic programming to find maximum sum_a for subsets of size k with sum_b >= total_a
dp = [{} for _ in range(n + 1)]
dp[0][0] = 0

for ai, bi in zip(a, b):
    # Iterate backwards to prevent overwriting
    for j in range(n, 0, -1):
        if j - 1 < 0:
            continue
        current = dp[j - 1]
        for sum_b in list(current.keys()):
            new_sum_b = sum_b + bi
            new_sum_a = current[sum_b] + ai
            if new_sum_b in dp[j]:
                if new_sum_a > dp[j][new_sum_b]:
                    dp[j][new_sum_b] = new_sum_a
            else:
                dp[j][new_sum_b] = new_sum_a

# Find the maximum sum_a for valid sum_b in dp[k]
max_sum_a = -1
for sum_b, sum_a in dp[k].items():
    if sum_b >= total_a and sum_a > max_sum_a:
        max_sum_a = sum_a

t = total_a - max_sum_a

但这对于n=5000来说，每个测试用例可能需要5000次检查，每次需要再遍历n-1个tetrapods, each requiring to collect the four points. 这对于5000这个数，在极端情况下如每个数据集都如此，计算量可能是5000*5000=25,000,000，这应该可以接受.

But the main problem is storage and efficient checking. Thus, to handle this effectively, let's think of a way to track these.

First, for the original case's validity check:

We can collect all squares into a map, or a hashset (with coordinate pairs), count hits. but for big n (5000), each with 4 squares, total of 20000 entries. So the check is manageable, since we can't have duplicate collisions except possibly globally.

For each data set, the first check is O(n) per permission for the computations.

Then, when checking whether removing one i:

We need to exclude all squares from tet i, and ensure the remaining squares are all unique.

To do this efficiently:

We can precompute:

For each tet i:

   Compute its four squares, store as a collection.

Also, have a global frequency map counting the occurrence of each square in the entire set. ( counts each square's occurrences in all four squares)

Then:

For the check with removing tet i, the counts for all squares in tet i’s four squares will decrease by one.

Thus:

The algorithm can be optimized as follows:

Precompute:

- For all tet rapods:

   collect their four points.

   For all points in the four of the tet, put into a list for the tet.

   also, in a frequency dictionary (like a hashmap or a dictionary where key is coordinate pair, value is count.

   populate this global frequency map initially by adding each instance in all four squares of all tet.

Then:

original validity is whether the maximum frequency in the map is 1. Because if any frequency >=2, then it's invalid.

But actually, the entire dataset is valid if all frequencies are exactly 1.

Because, the total count of squares should be 4n, and the sum over all counts would be 4n.

Thus, for original validity, check that all counts are exactly 1.

If not, then proceed to removal checks.

Now, for each candidate i (tet number to remove):

   The idea is to remove all four squares of the removed tet from the frequency map, check if the total remaining has all counts 1.

   But this is not efficient as simulating— because if done naively, for 5000 tets, each requires processing 4 squares and checking the entire map whether all are now 1, it's too slow.

Alternative approach:

We can track the frequency of each coordinate.

Precompute all the coordinates and frequencies.

Then, when considering remove ‘i’, the i's coordinates are s1,s2,s3,s4.

If any of these squares had frequency exactly 1 before, when removed, their frequency goes to 0.

Others (with frequency≥2), when subtract 1, will then be 1 (some could be down to 1, but others might still be 2).

Thus, the result after removal is valid only if after decrementing all of i's four squares by 1, every coordinate has frequency 0 or 1.

Also, we must ensure that no coordinate has frequency left at 2 or more.

But even computing this requires checking the entire frequency map's entries—which is O (40000) per candidate i— which for n=5000 is 5000*40000 → which is way too large.

So this approach may not be efficient enough.

So we need a better way.

Here's another idea:

Let's precompute for each tet i, the set of its four squares.

Let’s have the global frequency counts, as above.

Then, to check if removing i would yield a valid solution:

   The total remaining squares are 4*(n-1).

   The required condition is that any coordinate that is not intet i must appear exactly once.

   The coordinates in tet i may now have their counts decreased by one:

   for any coordinate that is in tet j's squares (other than i), their counts are intact except if the coordinate was in tetj and i both.

   So the idea is to track variables:

   For removing 'i', the only possible way validity could be met is if:

   1. The fourth square of any other tet is not the same as any of i的四个坐标, unless their count was exactly 1 before.

   Wait this seems messy.

Alternatively, let's compute for each candidate i:

the problematic overlaps introduced by the ‘i's squares. So, for the removal of ‘i’ to be valid, all coordinates except those in ‘i’s four must have their counts (in total excluding i's influence) equal to 1.

Thus:

The steps for checking removable i:

   Create a new frequency map by subtracting all frequencies of the four squares in i by one.

   Then, check that all frequ‌encies remaining are ≤1, and all are at least 0.

But this requires for each candidate ‘i’ to do O(4) modifications and then to check the frequencies for all coordinates. O(40000) per trial.

That would be 5000 * 40000 = ~200 million operations.

But that's way too much.

n, m = map(int, input().split())
cyclic = [input().strip() for _ in range(n)]
r, c = map(int, input().split())
pattern = [input().strip() for _ in range(r)]

constraints = []
for x in range(r):
    for y in range(c):
        v = pattern[x][y]
        if v != '?':
            constraints.append((x, y, v))

if not constraints:
    for _ in range(n):
        print('1' * m)
    exit()

result = [[1]*m for _ in range(n)]

for dx, dy, v in constraints:
    mask = [[0]*m for _ in range(n)]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(m):
            if cyclic[i][j] == v:
                ni = (i - dx) % n
                nj = (j - dy) % m
                mask[ni][nj] = 1
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(m):

This seems to hold universally. Therefore, the conclusion is that all solutions are pairs where P(x) is a monomial with a positive integer coefficient and non-negative integer exponent, and Q(x) is a linear polynomial determined by the exponent and the f-value of the coefficient.

Therefore, the answer is all pairs of integer polynomials P(x) = c x^k and Q(x) = k x + f(c), where c is a positive integer, k is a non-negative integer, and f(c) is the sum of the exponents in the prime factorization of c.

But the problem asks to "Find all pairs of integer polynomials P(x) and Q(x)" such that for any m, f(P(m)) = Q(f(m)). So the answer is that all such pairs are of the form P(x) = c x^k and Q(x) = k x + f(c), where c is a positive integer and k is a non-negative integer.

But we need to confirm that there are no other polynomials beyond monomials. For example, what if P(x) is a sum of monomials? Suppose P(x) = x + x^2. Let's see. Then f(m + m²). But this depends on m and m² +m. For example, m=2: m +m²=6, f(6)=2. Q(f(2))=Q(1). m=3: m +m²=12, f(12)=3. Q(f(3))=Q(1). So if Q(1) must be both 2 and 3, which is impossible. Hence, such P(x) cannot work. Therefore, only monomials are possible.

Similarly, other non-monomial polynomials would likely lead to inconsistencies as the value of f(P(m)) can't be expressed as a function of f(m) via a polynomial. Therefore, the only solutions are monomials P(x) = c x^k with corresponding Q(x)=k x + f(c).

Therefore, the final answer is all pairs where P(x) = c x^k and Q(x) = k x + f(c), for some positive integer c and non-negative integer k.

But the problem specifies integer polynomials, so c must be a positive integer, k a non-negative integer. But in the problem statement, when n=1, f(n)=0. So when c=1, f(c)=0, which is correct.

Thus, all solutions are pairs (P, Q) where P(x) is a monomial c x^k with c ∈ ℕ⁺ and k ∈ ℕ, and Q(x) is the linear polynomial k x + f(c). 

Therefore, the boxed answer should reflect this.

However, the problem asks to present the answer as pairs P(x) and Q(x). Therefore, the answer is all pairs where P(x) is a monomial with a positive integer coefficient and non-negative integer exponent, and Q(x) is the corresponding linear polynomial.

**Final Answer**
All such pairs are given by \( P(x) = c x^k \) and \( Q(x) = kx + f(c) \) where \( c \) is a positive integer and \( k \) is a non-negative integer. Therefore, the solutions are \boxed{P(x) = c x^k \text{ and } Q(x) = kx + f(c)}.
</think>To solve the problem of finding all pairs of integer polynomials \( P(x) \) and \( Q(x) \) such that for any positive integer \( m \), the equation \( f(P(m)) = Q(f(m)) \) holds, we need to carefully analyze the behavior of the function \( f \) and the structure of the polynomials \( P(x) \) and \( Q(x) \).

### Step-by-Step Solution:

1. **Definition of \( f(n) \)**:
   - For \( n = 1 \), \( f(1) = 0 \).
   - For \( n > 1 \), if \( n = p_1^{\alpha_1} p_2^{\alpha_2} \cdots p_k^{\alpha_k} \), then \( f(n) = \alpha_1 + \alpha_2 + \cdots + \alpha_k \).

2. **Constant Polynomials**:
   - If \( P(x) \) is a constant polynomial \( c \), then \( P(m) = c \) for all \( m \).
   - Therefore, \( f(P(m)) = f(c) \).
   - For \( Q(f(m)) \) to be equal to \( f(c) \) for all \( m \), \( Q(x) \) must be the constant polynomial \( f(c) \).
   - Specifically:
     - If \( c = 1 \), then \( Q(x) = 0 \).
     - If \( c \) is a prime \( p \), then \( Q(x) = 1 \).

3. **Monomial Polynomials**:
   - Consider \( P(x) = x^k \) for some non-negative integer \( k \).
   - Then \( P(m) = m^k \).
   - For any \( m \), \( f(m^k) = k \cdot f(m) \).
   - Therefore, \( Q(f(m)) = k \cdot f(m) \).
   - This implies \( Q(x) = kx \).

4. **Linear Polynomials with Prime Coefficients**:
   - Consider \( P(x) = px \) where \( p \) is a prime.
   - Then \( P(m) = pm \).
   - For any \( m \), \( f(pm) = f(p) + f(m) = 1 + f(m) \).
   - Therefore, \( Q(f(m)) = 1 + f(m) \).
   - This implies \( Q(x) = x + 1 \).

5. **General Monomials with Prime Power Coefficients**:
   - Consider \( P(x) = p^k x^l \) where \( p \) is a prime and \( k, l \) are non-negative integers.
   - Then \( P(m) = p^k m^l \).
   - For any \( m \), \( f(p^k m^l) = k + l \cdot f(m) \).
   - Therefore, \( Q(f(m)) = k + l \cdot f(m) \).
   - This implies \( Q(x) = lx + k \).

6. **General Case**:
   - For any positive integer \( c \) and non-negative integer \( k \), consider \( P(x) = c x^k \).
   - Then \( P(m) = c m^k \).
   - For any \( m \), \( f(c m^k) = f(c) + k \cdot f(m) \).
   - Therefore, \( Q(f(m)) = f(c) + k \cdot f(m) \).
   - This implies \( Q(x) = kx + f(c) \).

### Final Answer:
The pairs of integer polynomials \( P(x) \) and \( Q(x) \) that satisfy the given condition are of the form:
\[ P(x) = c x^k \]
\[ Q(x) = kx + f(c) \]
where \( c \) is a positive integer and \( k \) is a non-negative integer.

\[
\boxed{P(x) = c x^k \text{ and } Q(x) = kx + f(c)}

 如下：

 def check_remove_possible ( i, n, all_coords分组 by tet的 four squares):

   total = 0

   seen = set()

   for j from 0 to n-1:

      if j == i: continue 

      squares = tets [j]的 4四个 squares

      for sq in squares:

          if sq in seen:

               return False

          seen.add( sq )

   returns True if all were unique.

这样， the complexity is 5000 * (5000 *4) = but 5000* 20,000 → 1e8 operations, which might be feasible with optimized code in C++.

</think>To evaluate the integral \(\int \frac{e^x}{x^2} \, dx\), we will use integration by parts. Let's choose \(u\) and \(dv\) as follows:

\[
u = e^x \quad \text{and} \quad dv = \frac{1}{x^2} \, dx
\]

Then, we compute \(du\) and \(v\):

\[
du = e^x \, dx \quad \text{and} \quad v = \int \frac{1}{x^2} \, dx = -\frac{1}{x}
\]

Using the integration by parts formula \(\int u \, dv = uv - \int v \, du\), we get:

\[
\int \frac{e^x}{x^2} \, dx = e^x \left(-\frac{1}{x}\right) - \int \left(-\frac{1}{x}\right) e^x \, dx
\]

Simplifying this, we have:

\[
\int \frac{e^x}{x^2} \, dx = -\frac{e^x}{x} + \int \frac{e^x}{x} \, dx
\]

The remaining integral \(\int \frac{e^x}{x} \, dx\) is the exponential integral function, denoted by \(\text{Ei}(x)\). Therefore, the solution is:

\[
\int \frac{e^x}{x^2} \, dx = -\frac{e^x}{x} + \text{Ei}(x) + C
\]

where \(C\) is the constant of integration. Thus, the final answer is:

\[
\boxed{-\frac{e^x}{x} + \text{Ei}(x) + C}
\]<#sep#>human: There are n cities and n − 1 roads, which are trees. Cities are numbered from 1 to n. When city 1 is taken as the root, the parent of city i is pi, and the distance between i and pi is di. Sunuke wants to solve the following problem for each k of 1 or more and n or less.

n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))
min_time = float('inf')

for k in range(n + 1):
    if k == 0:
        current = 10**6 - a[0]
    elif k == n:
        current = a[-1] - 1
    else:
        my_time = a[k-1] - 1
        friend_time = 10**6 - a[k]
        current = max(my_time, friend_time)
    if current < min_time:
        min_time = current
